# SM4n't
> CTF: **CyberCamp Speedrun 2026** <br>
> Category: **Crypto**

## Description
```
They told me SM4 was good enough.
I didn't have time to read the standard, so I made my own version.
It has blocks, an S-box, XORs.

What could possibly go wrong?
```

## Goals
```
Find the key
```

## Attachments
```py
from secrets import FLAG
from pwn import xor

BLOCK = 8
pt = b"SRDNLEN_SPEEDRUN_2026_CHALLENGE!"
assert len(FLAG) == 32
assert len(pt) == len(FLAG)

SBOX = (
    0x63, 0x7C, 0x77, 0x7B, 0xF2, 0x6B, 0x6F, 0xC5, 0x30, 0x01, 0x67, 0x2B, 0xFE, 0xD7, 0xAB, 0x76,
    0xCA, 0x82, 0xC9, 0x7D, 0xFA, 0x59, 0x47, 0xF0, 0xAD, 0xD4, 0xA2, 0xAF, 0x9C, 0xA4, 0x72, 0xC0,
    0xB7, 0xFD, 0x93, 0x26, 0x36, 0x3F, 0xF7, 0xCC, 0x34, 0xA5, 0xE5, 0xF1, 0x71, 0xD8, 0x31, 0x15,
    0x04, 0xC7, 0x23, 0xC3, 0x18, 0x96, 0x05, 0x9A, 0x07, 0x12, 0x80, 0xE2, 0xEB, 0x27, 0xB2, 0x75,
    0x09, 0x83, 0x2C, 0x1A, 0x1B, 0x6E, 0x5A, 0xA0, 0x52, 0x3B, 0xD6, 0xB3, 0x29, 0xE3, 0x2F, 0x84,
    0x53, 0xD1, 0x00, 0xED, 0x20, 0xFC, 0xB1, 0x5B, 0x6A, 0xCB, 0xBE, 0x39, 0x4A, 0x4C, 0x58, 0xCF,
    0xD0, 0xEF, 0xAA, 0xFB, 0x43, 0x4D, 0x33, 0x85, 0x45, 0xF9, 0x02, 0x7F, 0x50, 0x3C, 0x9F, 0xA8,
    0x51, 0xA3, 0x40, 0x8F, 0x92, 0x9D, 0x38, 0xF5, 0xBC, 0xB6, 0xDA, 0x21, 0x10, 0xFF, 0xF3, 0xD2,
    0xCD, 0x0C, 0x13, 0xEC, 0x5F, 0x97, 0x44, 0x17, 0xC4, 0xA7, 0x7E, 0x3D, 0x64, 0x5D, 0x19, 0x73,
    0x60, 0x81, 0x4F, 0xDC, 0x22, 0x2A, 0x90, 0x88, 0x46, 0xEE, 0xB8, 0x14, 0xDE, 0x5E, 0x0B, 0xDB,
    0xE0, 0x32, 0x3A, 0x0A, 0x49, 0x06, 0x24, 0x5C, 0xC2, 0xD3, 0xAC, 0x62, 0x91, 0x95, 0xE4, 0x79,
    0xE7, 0xC8, 0x37, 0x6D, 0x8D, 0xD5, 0x4E, 0xA9, 0x6C, 0x56, 0xF4, 0xEA, 0x65, 0x7A, 0xAE, 0x08,
    0xBA, 0x78, 0x25, 0x2E, 0x1C, 0xA6, 0xB4, 0xC6, 0xE8, 0xDD, 0x74, 0x1F, 0x4B, 0xBD, 0x8B, 0x8A,
    0x70, 0x3E, 0xB5, 0x66, 0x48, 0x03, 0xF6, 0x0E, 0x61, 0x35, 0x57, 0xB9, 0x86, 0xC1, 0x1D, 0x9E,
    0xE1, 0xF8, 0x98, 0x11, 0x69, 0xD9, 0x8E, 0x94, 0x9B, 0x1E, 0x87, 0xE9, 0xCE, 0x55, 0x28, 0xDF,
    0x8C, 0xA1, 0x89, 0x0D, 0xBF, 0xE6, 0x42, 0x68, 0x41, 0x99, 0x2D, 0x0F, 0xB0, 0x54, 0xBB, 0x16,
)

def substitute(msg): 
    return bytes([SBOX[b] for b in msg])
    
def split(msg):
    return [msg[i:i+BLOCK] for i in range(0, len(msg), BLOCK)]

def sm4(pt, key): 
    ct, key = split(pt), split(key)

    for i in range(len(key)): 
        ks = xor(*ct[1:],[key[i]])
        ks = substitute(ks)
        block = xor(ks, ct[0])
        ct = ct[1:] + [block]

    return (b"".join(ct)).hex()

open("ct.txt", "w").write("Ciphertext: " + sm4(pt, FLAG)) # Ciphertext: e11808041f506915277b4c348eee513cd663605cfe54d0d7678b17e8040398cd
```

## How Code Work
The idea of the code, consist to implement the SM4 encryption, that consist in:
1. we xor the first 3 block after the block that we are encrypting;
2. the result we encrypt it with the key;
3. the result of the encryption we xor it with the block that we are encrypting;
4. than we shift the three block by one, and we put at lest the result of the last xor

Simple using this feistel to encrypt a plaintext that have length 4 block. 

And with the code we encrypt every block of the plaintext and put it at the last position. 

## Exploit
Reviewing the code, we can see that the for it goes only 4 times, that is the same amount of the block that we have of the code, the plaintext and the ciphertext.

Since the number of iterations is small, we can write a description of what each block does: 
- $C_0$: $SUB(pt_1 \oplus pt_2 \oplus pt_3 \oplus key_0) \oplus pt_0$
- $C_1$: $SUB(pt_2 \oplus pt_3 \oplus C_0  \oplus key_1) \oplus pt_1$
- $C_2$: $SUB(pt_3 \oplus C_0 \oplus C_1 \oplus key_2) \oplus pt_2$
- $C_3$: $SUB(C_0 \oplus C_1 \oplus C_2 \oplus key_3) \oplus pt_3$

Having only 4 block, at the end of the encryption, we actually have the block in order.

Now that we have the encryption of every iteration, we can now analyze from the side of an attacker, and we can actualy see that we can simple reverse it because we have: 
- Substitution block;
- Every block of plaintext;
- Every block of ciphertext;

In the end, to find the flag, that is the key, we simple need to do this 4 operations:
- $key_0$: $RevSUB(C_0 \oplus pt_0) \oplus (pt_1 \oplus pt_2 \oplus pt_3)$
- $key_1$: $RevSUB(C_1 \oplus pt_1) \oplus (pt_2 \oplus pt_3 \oplus C_0)$
- $key_2$: $RevSUB(C_2 \oplus pt_2) \oplus (pt_3 \oplus C_0 \oplus C_1)$
- $key_3$: $RevSUB(C_3 \oplus pt_3) \oplus (C_0 \oplus C_1 \oplus C_2)$

### Unintended Exploit
We can actually also bruteforce the key, where every time we try every combination, and sending every time '\x00' for padding
